It’s not like I sat down and decided not to eat, or I’m trying to lose weight. It just happens.\n\nWhat really got my attention is I hardly eat right anymore. Some days I’ll have coffee and that’s basically it for a long while, and then maybe later I’ll have a piece of bread or something small because I know I need something in my stomach. And then I notice my pants hanging loose on me, like I’m constantly pulling them up, and that makes it feel more real.\n\nSo yeah, I guess it must be related to whatever’s going on with me, because this isn’t how I normally am. But I can’t point to one thing and say, “That’s why.” It’s more like my mind just isn’t interested in food right now.\n\nDoctor: Are there other things you used to enjoy that you don't find pleasurable anymore?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, there are. I used to like walking by the lake. Not even anything big, just going there and taking my time, watching the water, hearing the birds, feeling like I could breathe a little. I used to look forward to that, like it was one of the few simple things that made me feel okay.\n\nAnd I had these little model kits too—nothing fancy, just stuff I’d work on with my hands. I’d sit there lining up the pieces, painting small parts, taking my time with it. It used to calm me down. But now they just sit there. I see them on the table or in the corner, and it’s like… I don’t know, like they belong to somebody else. Like I recognize that I used to care about them, but I can’t reach that feeling anymore.\n\nI’ll think, “Maybe I should go walk,” or “Maybe I should pick up one of those kits,” but then I just don’t. There’s no pleasure in the thought of it. It all feels kind of flat.\n\nDoctor: Are there other things besides food and the activities you mentioned, like walking or model kits, that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, the main ones that come to mind are still those same things, honestly. The walking by the lake and the model kits, because those were sort of my things. I didn’t have a whole big list of hobbies, you know? It wasn’t like I was out doing all kinds of stuff before. But those mattered to me.\n\nThe lake especially… I used to like just being there. I’d go and walk slow, sometimes sit for a while, just look at the water. It sounds small, but it used to give me something. And the little model kits, I’d have them set up and I could spend a long time on one tiny part, and it felt satisfying when it came together.\n\nNow they just sit there. I’ll see the kits sitting around and it’s like they belong to somebody else. Like I know I’m the one who bought them, I know I used to enjoy doing them, but I don’t feel pulled toward them anymore. Same with walking—I can think about it, but I don’t really want to go. It’s like the enjoyment part is missing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you feel like doing those things you used to enjoy, like walking by the lake or working on your model kits?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’ll have the thought sometimes, like, “You used to go down to the lake, maybe you should do that,” or I’ll see the model kits sitting there and think maybe I should pick one up. But it’s not the same as wanting to do it.\n\nBefore, I would actually feel something about it, like the idea of walking by the water would feel kind of nice in my head before I even went. Or with the kits, I’d get interested in lining things up or figuring out the little pieces. Now it’s more like I’m just remembering that I used to be a person who liked those things.\n\nThey just sit there now. The model kits especially… I look at them and it’s strange, like they belong to somebody else, even though I know they’re mine. And the lake is the same way in my mind. I know I used to like walking there, but I don’t really get that pull to go. So no, I don’t really have days where I feel like doing them. It’s more just me knowing I should maybe try, but there’s nothing behind it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or empty inside?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, honestly. It’s not always like crying sad, if that makes sense. Sometimes it’s more just this heavy, empty feeling, like I wake up and before anything even happens, it’s already sitting there in my chest.\n\nIt feels like there’s nothing good waiting for me. Like the day is already kind of… blank, or weighed down, before I even get out of bed. I just want to keep my head down and not deal with much. It’s hard to explain, because “sad” sounds like one thing, but for me it’s more like everything inside feels hollow and heavy at the same time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep has been pretty bad lately. It’s like I’m tired, but then when I actually get in bed, my body won’t just shut off. I’ll lie there for hours sometimes, just staring at the wall or the ceiling, turning over, trying to get comfortable, and nothing really happens. It gets quiet and dark, and I’m just… awake.\n\nAnd then other times, even if I do fall asleep, I wake up way too early, like before the sun is even up, and I just can’t get back to sleep. I’ll keep thinking maybe if I stay still long enough I’ll drift off again, but I don’t. I just lay there knowing the whole day is going to start and I already feel worn out before it even begins.\n\nEven on nights where I technically slept some, it doesn’t feel like it helped much. I get up feeling like I barely rested at all. It’s frustrating, because I want to sleep, I really do, but it’s either taking forever to come or it doesn’t last.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you might enjoy those things again, like walking by the lake or working on your model kits, or does it feel like that part of you is gone for good?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I want to say maybe, because I remember that I used to be that way. Like, I remember walking by the lake and it actually meaning something to me, just being there by the water and having that quiet feeling. And I remember sitting with those little model kits, messing with the pieces, taking my time with them. So part of me knows that was real.\n\nBut right now it feels very far away. It doesn’t feel like, “Oh, I’ll get back to that soon.” It feels more like I’m looking at proof that I used to care about things. The kits are still sitting there, and I’ll see them and think, “Those are mine,” but they almost feel like they belong to somebody else. Like someone else bought them, someone else had the patience for them, someone else got something out of them.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d say gone for good… that sounds too final, and I guess I don’t want to believe that. But it does feel gone right now. I don’t feel the pull toward any of it. I just remember that I used to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about five months, I think. Not just a bad week or something that comes and goes for a day or two. This whole spell has kind of been hanging over me for around five months now.\n\nI didn’t mark it on a calendar or anything, but when I think back, that’s about when I started noticing that the things I used to do just weren’t pulling me in anymore. At first I thought maybe I was just tired or in a rut, and I kept thinking it would pass if I waited it out. But it’s stayed. Like month after month, it’s still been there, that feeling of not really caring about the things I used to enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, yes. It’s not always like I’m crying or anything like that, but I wake up and there’s just this heavy, empty feeling sitting in my chest right away. Like before anything has even happened, it already feels like the day is going to be something I have to get through.\n\nIt’s hard to explain, because it’s not one clear thought all the time. It’s more like this weight, like nothing good is really waiting for me. I’ll open my eyes and I just feel… low. Empty. Like I want to keep my head down and not really deal with anything or anyone.\n\nAnd that feeling stays with me for most of the day. Sometimes it might fade into the background a little, but it doesn’t really leave. It’s just there, like I’m carrying it around.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I just remember that I used to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about five months, I think. Not just a bad week or something that comes and goes for a day or two. This whole spell has kind of been hanging over me for around five months now.\n\nI didn’t mark it on a calendar or anything, but when I think back, that’s about when I started noticing that the things I used to do just weren’t pulling me in anymore. At first I thought maybe I was just tired or in a rut, and I kept thinking it would pass if I waited it out. But it’s stayed. Like month after month, it’s still been there, that feeling of not really caring about the things I used to enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, yes. It’s not always like I’m crying or anything like that, but I wake up and there’s just this heavy, empty feeling sitting in my chest right away. Like before anything has even happened, it already feels like the day is going to be something I have to get through.\n\nIt’s hard to explain, because it’s not one clear thought all the time. It’s more like this weight, like nothing good is really waiting for me. I’ll open my eyes and I just feel… low. Empty. Like I want to keep my head down and not really deal with anything or anyone.\n\nAnd that feeling stays with me for most of the day. Sometimes it might fade into the background a little, but it doesn’t really leave. It’s just there, like I’m carrying it around.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel like you have a lot of energy most days?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No… not really. Most days it feels like I barely have any energy at all. Even little things feel way bigger than they should.

Like taking out the trash — that sounds like nothing, right? It’s just tying the bag, walking it out, coming back in. But I’ll stand there looking at it like it’s this whole job I don’t know if I can do. Or going to the clinic, even when I know I need to, it feels like too much before I even start getting ready.

It’s like my body is heavy in a way that’s hard to explain. My arms and legs feel like they’re full of wet sand, like every movement takes more effort than it should. I’m not talking about being a little tired after doing something. It’s more like I wake up already drained, and then everything I’m supposed to do just sits there waiting on me.

    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess the main thing is my eating has gotten really bad. Like, I just don’t eat right anymore. Some days I’ll go the whole day with just coffee, and maybe later I’ll have a piece of bread or something small like that, and that’s it. It’s not like I’m trying to lose weight or anything, it just… food doesn’t really happen.\n\nAnd I can tell it’s showing, because my pants are hanging loose on me now. I keep having to pull them up, and I notice it every time I get dressed. It’s been worrying me, because I know that’s not normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I’m not sitting there scared I’ll gain weight or trying to keep it down. It’s more like I just don’t eat right anymore. Some days it’s just coffee, and then maybe later I’ll have a piece of bread or something small, and then the day is over before I really ate anything.\n\nAnd I notice it because my pants are getting loose, like hanging on me, and I keep having to pull them up. That part worries me, but it’s not because I want to be thinner or anything. It just feels like eating doesn’t happen the way it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy eating when you do eat?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I’d say I enjoy it much lately, because half the time I barely get around to eating at all. It’s not like I sit down to a meal and think, “Oh, this tastes good,” the way I used to. A lot of days it’s just coffee, then maybe I realize later I should put something in my stomach, so I’ll eat a piece of bread or something small like that.\n\nAnd then I notice my pants are loose, like actually hanging on me, and I keep having to pull them up. That’s what makes me think something’s really off. It’s not that I’m avoiding food because I don’t like it exactly… it’s more like eating just doesn’t really happen, and when it does, it’s not much of an enjoyable thing.\n\nDoctor: Are you ever worried about eating because of what might happen when you eat, like choking or vomiting?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like choking or throwing up, no. I mean, I can’t remember sitting there worried that if I eat something bad will happen like that. It’s not that kind of fear, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nIt’s more just… I don’t get to it, or I don’t feel like dealing with it, and then by the time I notice, I’ve barely had anything. But no, I’m not avoiding food because I think I’ll choke or vomit.\n\nDoctor: How much weight would you say you've lost since this started?\n\nPatient: I don’t know the exact number, honestly. I haven’t been weighing myself or anything like that. I just know my clothes fit different—my pants are really loose now, like they hang on me, and I’m always pulling them up.\n\nIt’s been enough that I notice it every day, but I couldn’t tell you, like, “I lost ten pounds” or whatever. It’s more that since I started barely eating—some days just coffee, maybe a piece of bread later—it feels like the weight’s coming off whether I mean for it to or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no appetite most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the time I really don’t have much of an appetite. It’s like food just doesn’t call to me anymore, if that makes sense. I’ll get through a whole part of the day and then realize all I’ve had is coffee, and maybe later I’ll force down something small, like a piece of bread, just because I know I probably should.\n\nIt’s not like I’m planning it or trying to not eat. I just… hardly eat right anymore. And it’s gotten noticeable because my pants are hanging loose on me now, like I keep having to pull them up. So yeah, I’d say most of the time my appetite is pretty much gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in food in general?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. I wouldn’t say food disgusts me or anything like that, it’s just… I don’t really think about it much, and when I do, it feels like a chore. Like something I’m supposed to do, not something I want.\n\nThat’s kind of what brought me in, honestly. I hardly eat right anymore. Some days it’s just coffee, and then maybe way later I’ll have a piece of bread or something small, just to have something in me. And I keep noticing my pants hanging loose, like they don’t fit the same, so it’s not just in my head. I’m not trying to make that happen, I just don’t seem interested in food like I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in food is related to anything going on in your life or your mind?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know exactly what it’s tied to. It feels like something in me just shut off around food. Like I’ll think, “I should probably eat,” but there’s no pull to actually do it. It’s not like I sat down and decided not to eat, or I’m trying to lose weight. It just happens.\n\nWhat really got my attention is I hardly eat right anymore. Some days I’ll have coffee and that’s basically it for a long while, and then maybe later I’ll have a piece of bread or something small because I know I need something in my stomach. And then I notice my pants hanging loose on me, like I’m constantly pulling them up, and that makes it feel more real.\n\nSo yeah, I guess it must be related to whatever’s going on with me, because this isn’t how I normally am. But I can’t point to one thing and say, “That’s why.” It’s more like my mind just isn’t interested in food right now.\n\nDoctor: Are there other things you used to enjoy that you don't find pleasurable anymore?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, there are. I used to like walking by the lake. Not even anything big, just going there and taking my time, watching the water, hearing the birds, feeling like I could breathe a little. I used to look forward to that, like it was one of the few simple things that made me feel okay.\n\nAnd I had these little model kits too—nothing fancy, just stuff I’d work on with my hands. I’d sit there lining up the pieces, painting small parts, taking my time with it. It used to calm me down. But now they just sit there. I see them on the table or in the corner, and it’s like… I don’t know, like they belong to somebody else. Like I recognize that I used to care about them, but I can’t reach that feeling anymore.\n\nI’ll think, “Maybe I should go walk,” or “Maybe I should pick up one of those kits,” but then I just don’t. There’s no pleasure in the thought of it. It all feels kind of flat.\n\nDoctor: Are there other things besides food and the activities you mentioned, like walking or model kits, that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, the main ones that come to mind are still those same things, honestly. The walking by the lake and the model kits, because those were sort of my things. I didn’t have a whole big list of hobbies, you know? It wasn’t like I was out doing all kinds of stuff before. But those mattered to me.\n\nThe lake especially… I used to like just being there. I’d go and walk slow, sometimes sit for a while, just look at the water. It sounds small, but it used to give me something. And the little model kits, I’d have them set up and I could spend a long time on one tiny part, and it felt satisfying when it came together.\n\nNow they just sit there. I’ll see the kits sitting around and it’s like they belong to somebody else. Like I know I’m the one who bought them, I know I used to enjoy doing them, but I don’t feel pulled toward them anymore. Same with walking—I can think about it, but I don’t really want to go. It’s like the enjoyment part is missing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you feel like doing those things you used to enjoy, like walking by the lake or working on your model kits?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’ll have the thought sometimes, like, “You used to go down to the lake, maybe you should do that,” or I’ll see the model kits sitting there and think maybe I should pick one up. But it’s not the same as wanting to do it.\n\nBefore, I would actually feel something about it, like the idea of walking by the water would feel kind of nice in my head before I even went. Or with the kits, I’d get interested in lining things up or figuring out the little pieces. Now it’s more like I’m just remembering that I used to be a person who liked those things.\n\nThey just sit there now. The model kits especially… I look at them and it’s strange, like they belong to somebody else, even though I know they’re mine. And the lake is the same way in my mind. I know I used to like walking there, but I don’t really get that pull to go. So no, I don’t really have days where I feel like doing them. It’s more just me knowing I should maybe try, but there’s nothing behind it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or empty inside?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, honestly. It’s not always like crying sad, if that makes sense. Sometimes it’s more just this heavy, empty feeling, like I wake up and before anything even happens, it’s already sitting there in my chest.\n\nIt feels like there’s nothing good waiting for me. Like the day is already kind of… blank, or weighed down, before I even get out of bed. I just want to keep my head down and not deal with much. It’s hard to explain, because “sad” sounds like one thing, but for me it’s more like everything inside feels hollow and heavy at the same time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep has been pretty bad lately. It’s like I’m tired, but then when I actually get in bed, my body won’t just shut off. I’ll lie there for hours sometimes, just staring at the wall or the ceiling, turning over, trying to get comfortable, and nothing really happens. It gets quiet and dark, and I’m just… awake.\n\nAnd then other times, even if I do fall asleep, I wake up way too early, like before the sun is even up, and I just can’t get back to sleep. I’ll keep thinking maybe if I stay still long enough I’ll drift off again, but I don’t. I just lay there knowing the whole day is going to start and I already feel worn out before it even begins.\n\nEven on nights where I technically slept some, it doesn’t feel like it helped much. I get up feeling like I barely rested at all. It’s frustrating, because I want to sleep, I really do, but it’s either taking forever to come or it doesn’t last.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you might enjoy those things again, like walking by the lake or working on your model kits, or does it feel like that part of you is gone for good?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I want to say maybe, because I remember that I used to be that way. Like, I remember walking by the lake and it actually meaning something to me, just being there by the water and having that quiet feeling. And I remember sitting with those little model kits, messing with the pieces, taking my time with them. So part of me knows that was real.\n\nBut right now it feels very far away. It doesn’t feel like, “Oh, I’ll get back to that soon.” It feels more like I’m looking at proof that I used to care about things. The kits are still sitting there, and I’ll see them and think, “Those are mine,” but they almost feel like they belong to somebody else. Like someone else bought them, someone else had the patience for them, someone else got something out of them.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d say gone for good… that sounds too final, and I guess I don’t want to believe that. But it does feel gone right now. I don’t feel the pull toward any of it. I just remember that I used to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about five months, I think. Not just a bad week or something that comes and goes for a day or two. This whole spell has kind of been hanging over me for around five months now.\n\nI didn’t mark it on a calendar or anything, but when I think back, that’s about when I started noticing that the things I used to do just weren’t pulling me in anymore. At first I thought maybe I was just tired or in a rut, and I kept thinking it would pass if I waited it out. But it’s stayed. I’m not trying to make that happen, I just don’t seem interested in food like I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in food is related to anything going on in your life or your mind?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know exactly what it’s tied to. It feels like something in me just shut off around food. Like I’ll think, “I should probably eat,” but there’s no pull to actually do it. It’s not like I sat down and decided not to eat, or I’m trying to lose weight. It just happens.\n\nWhat really got my attention is I hardly eat right anymore. Some days I’ll have coffee and that’s basically it for a long while, and then maybe later I’ll have a piece of bread or something small because I know I need something in my stomach. And then I notice my pants hanging loose on me, like I’m constantly pulling them up, and that makes it feel more real.\n\nSo yeah, I guess it must be related to whatever’s going on with me, because this isn’t how I normally am. But I can’t point to one thing and say, “That’s why.” It’s more like my mind just isn’t interested in food right now.\n\nDoctor: Are there other things you used to enjoy that you don't find pleasurable anymore?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, there are. I used to like walking by the lake. Not even anything big, just going there and taking my time, watching the water, hearing the birds, feeling like I could breathe a little. I used to look forward to that, like it was one of the few simple things that made me feel okay.\n\nAnd I had these little model kits too—nothing fancy, just stuff I’d work on with my hands. I’d sit there lining up the pieces, painting small parts, taking my time with it. It used to calm me down. But now they just sit there. I see them on the table or in the corner, and it’s like… I don’t know, like they belong to somebody else. Like I recognize that I used to care about them, but I can’t reach that feeling anymore.\n\nI’ll think, “Maybe I should go walk,” or “Maybe I should pick up one of those kits,” but then I just don’t. There’s no pleasure in the thought of it. It all feels kind of flat.\n\nDoctor: Are there other things besides food and the activities you mentioned, like walking or model kits, that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, the main ones that come to mind are still those same things, honestly. The walking by the lake and the model kits, because those were sort of my things. I didn’t have a whole big list of hobbies, you know? It wasn’t like I was out doing all kinds of stuff before. But those mattered to me.\n\nThe lake especially… I used to like just being there. I’d go and walk slow, sometimes sit for a while, just look at the water. It sounds small, but it used to give me something. And the little model kits, I’d have them set up and I could spend a long time on one tiny part, and it felt satisfying when it came together.\n\nNow they just sit there. I’ll see the kits sitting around and it’s like they belong to somebody else. Like I know I’m the one who bought them, I know I used to enjoy doing them, but I don’t feel pulled toward them anymore. Same with walking—I can think about it, but I don’t really want to go. It’s like the enjoyment part is missing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you feel like doing those things you used to enjoy, like walking by the lake or working on your model kits?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’ll have the thought sometimes, like, “You used to go down to the lake, maybe you should do that,” or I’ll see the model kits sitting there and think maybe I should pick one up. But it’s not the same as wanting to do it.\n\nBefore, I would actually feel something about it, like the idea of walking by the water would feel kind of nice in my head before I even went. Or with the kits, I’d get interested in lining things up or figuring out the little pieces. Now it’s more like I’m just remembering that I used to be a person who liked those things.\n\nThey just sit there now. The model kits especially… I look at them and it’s strange, like they belong to somebody else, even though I know they’re mine. And the lake is the same way in my mind. I know I used to like walking there, but I don’t really get that pull to go. So no, I don’t really have days where I feel like doing them. It’s more just me knowing I should maybe try, but there’s nothing behind it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or empty inside?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, honestly. It’s not always like crying sad, if that makes sense. Sometimes it’s more just this heavy, empty feeling, like I wake up and before anything even happens, it’s already sitting there in my chest.\n\nIt feels like there’s nothing good waiting for me. Like the day is already kind of… blank, or weighed down, before I even get out of bed. I just want to keep my head down and not deal with much. It’s hard to explain, because “sad” sounds like one thing, but for me it’s more like everything inside feels hollow and heavy at the same time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep has been pretty bad lately. It’s like I’m tired, but then when I actually get in bed, my body won’t just shut off. I’ll lie there for hours sometimes, just staring at the wall or the ceiling, turning over, trying to get comfortable, and nothing really happens. It gets quiet and dark, and I’m just… awake.\n\nAnd then other times, even if I do fall asleep, I wake up way too early, like before the sun is even up, and I just can’t get back to sleep. I’ll keep thinking maybe if I stay still long enough I’ll drift off again, but I don’t. I just lay there knowing the whole day is going to start and I already feel worn out before it even begins.\n\nEven on nights where I technically slept some, it doesn’t feel like it helped much. I get up feeling like I barely rested at all. It’s frustrating, because I want to sleep, I really do, but it’s either taking forever to come or it doesn’t last.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you might enjoy those things again, like walking by the lake or working on your model kits, or does it feel like that part of you is gone for good?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I want to say maybe, because I remember that I used to be that way. Like, I remember walking by the lake and it actually meaning something to me, just being there by the water and having that quiet feeling. And I remember sitting with those little model kits, messing with the pieces, taking my time with them. So part of me knows that was real.\n\nBut right now it feels very far away. It doesn’t feel like, “Oh, I’ll get back to that soon.” It feels more like I’m looking at proof that I used to care about things. The kits are still sitting there, and I’ll see them and think, “Those are mine,” but they almost feel like they belong to somebody else. Like someone else bought them, someone else had the patience for them, someone else got something out of them.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d say gone for good… that sounds too final, and I guess I don’t want to believe that. But it does feel gone right now. I don’t feel the pull toward any of it. I just remember that I used to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about five months, I think. Not just a bad week or something that comes and goes for a day or two. This whole spell has kind of been hanging over me for around five months now.\n\nI didn’t mark it on a calendar or anything, but when I think back, that’s about when I started noticing that the things I used to do just weren’t pulling me in anymore. At first I thought maybe I was just tired or in a rut, and I kept thinking it would pass if I waited it out. But it’s stayed. Like month after month, it’s still been there, that feeling of not really caring about the things I used to enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, yes. It’s not always like I’m crying or anything like that, but I wake up and there’s just this heavy, empty feeling sitting in my chest right away. Like before anything has even happened, it already feels like the day is going to be something I have to get through.\n\nIt’s hard to explain, because it’s not one clear thought all the time. It’s more like this weight, like nothing good is really waiting for me. I’ll open my eyes and I just feel… low. Empty. Sometimes it might fade into the background a little, but it doesn’t really leave. Yeah… I think so. I don’t have a scale at home, so I can’t tell you the exact number, but I can see it in my clothes. My pants are loose on me now, like they kind of hang off my waist more than they used to.

It’s more that since I started barely eating—some days just coffee, maybe a piece of bread later—it feels like the weight’s coming off whether I mean for it to or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no appetite most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the time I really don’t have much of an appetite. It’s like food just doesn’t call to me anymore, if that makes sense. I’ll get through a whole part of the day and then realize all I’ve had is coffee, and maybe later I’ll force down something small, like a piece of bread, just because I know I probably should.\n\nIt’s not like I’m planning it or trying to not eat. I just… hardly eat right anymore. And it’s gotten noticeable because my pants are hanging loose on me now, like I keep having to pull them up. So yeah, I’d say most of the time my appetite is pretty much gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in food in general?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. I wouldn’t say food disgusts me or anything like that, it’s just… I don’t really think about it much, and when I do, it feels like a chore. Like something I’m supposed to do, not something I want.\n\nThat’s kind of what brought me in, honestly. I hardly eat right anymore. Some days it’s just coffee, and then maybe way later I’ll have a piece of bread or something small, just to have something in me. And I keep noticing my pants hanging loose, like they don’t fit the same, so it’s not just in my head. I’m not trying to make that happen, I just don’t seem interested in food like I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in food is related to anything going on in your life or your mind?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know exactly what it’s tied to. It feels like something in me just shut off around food. Like I’ll think, “I should probably eat,” but there’s no pull to actually do it. It’s not like I sat down and decided not to eat, or I’m trying to lose weight. It just happens.\n\nWhat really got my attention is I hardly eat right anymore. Some days I’ll have coffee and that’s basically it for a long while, and then maybe later I’ll have a piece of bread or something small because I know I need something in my stomach. And then I notice my pants hanging loose on me, like I’m constantly pulling them up, and that makes it feel more real.\n\nSo yeah, I guess it must be related to whatever’s going on with me, because this isn’t how I normally am. But I can’t point to one thing and say, “That’s why.” It’s more like my mind just isn’t interested in food right now.\n\nDoctor: Are there other things you used to enjoy that you don't find pleasurable anymore?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, there are. I used to like walking by the lake. Not even anything big, just going there and taking my time, watching the water, hearing the birds, feeling like I could breathe a little. I used to look forward to that, like it was one of the few simple things that made me feel okay.\n\nAnd I had these little model kits too—nothing fancy, just stuff I’d work on with my hands. I’d sit there lining up the pieces, painting small parts, taking my time with it. It used to calm me down. But now they just sit there. I see them on the table or in the corner, and it’s like… I don’t know, like they belong to somebody else. Like I recognize that I used to care about them, but I can’t reach that feeling anymore.\n\nI’ll think, “Maybe I should go walk,” or “Maybe I should pick up one of those kits,” but then I just don’t. There’s no pleasure in the thought of it. It all feels kind of flat.\n\nDoctor: Are there other things besides food and the activities you mentioned, like walking or model kits, that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, the main ones that come to mind are still those same things, honestly. The walking by the lake and the model kits, because those were sort of my things. I didn’t have a whole big list of hobbies, you know? It wasn’t like I was out doing all kinds of stuff before. But those mattered to me.\n\nThe lake especially… I used to like just being there. I’d go and walk slow, sometimes sit for a while, just look at the water. It sounds small, but it used to give me something. And the little model kits, I’d have them set up and I could spend a long time on one tiny part, and it felt satisfying when it came together.\n\nNow they just sit there. I’ll see the kits sitting around and it’s like they belong to somebody else. Like I know I’m the one who bought them, I know I used to enjoy doing them, but I don’t feel pulled toward them anymore. Same with walking—I can think about it, but I don’t really want to go. It’s like the enjoyment part is missing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you feel like doing those things you used to enjoy, like walking by the lake or working on your model kits?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’ll have the thought sometimes, like, “You used to go down to the lake, maybe you should do that,” or I’ll see the model kits sitting there and think maybe I should pick one up. But it’s not the same as wanting to do it.\n\nBefore, I would actually feel something about it, like the idea of walking by the water would feel kind of nice in my head before I even went. Or with the kits, I’d get interested in lining things up or figuring out the little pieces. Now it’s more like I’m just remembering that I used to be a person who liked those things.\n\nThey just sit there now. The model kits especially… I look at them and it’s strange, like they belong to somebody else, even though I know they’re mine. And the lake is the same way in my mind. I know I used to like walking there, but I don’t really get that pull to go. So no, I don’t really have days where I feel like doing them. It’s more just me knowing I should maybe try, but there’s nothing behind it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or empty inside?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, honestly. It’s not always like crying sad, if that makes sense. Sometimes it’s more just this heavy, empty feeling, like I wake up and before anything even happens, it’s already sitting there in my chest.\n\nIt feels like there’s nothing good waiting for me. Like the day is already kind of… blank, or weighed down, before I even get out of bed. I just want to keep my head down and not deal with much. It’s hard to explain, because “sad” sounds like one thing, but for me it’s more like everything inside feels hollow and heavy at the same time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep has been pretty bad lately. It’s like I’m tired, but then when I actually get in bed, my body won’t just shut off. I’ll lie there for hours sometimes, just staring at the wall or the ceiling, turning over, trying to get comfortable, and nothing really happens. It gets quiet and dark, and I’m just… awake.\n\nAnd then other times, even if I do fall asleep, I wake up way too early, like before the sun is even up, and I just can’t get back to sleep. I’ll keep thinking maybe if I stay still long enough I’ll drift off again, but I don’t. I just lay there knowing the whole day is going to start and I already feel worn out before it even begins.\n\nEven on nights where I technically slept some, it doesn’t feel like it helped much. I get up feeling like I barely rested at all. It’s frustrating, because I want to sleep, I really do, but it’s either taking forever to come or it doesn’t last.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you might enjoy those things again, like walking by the lake or working on your model kits, or does it feel like that part of you is gone for good?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I want to say maybe, because I remember that I used to be that way. Like, I remember walking by the lake and it actually meaning something to me, just being there by the water and having that quiet feeling. And I remember sitting with those little model kits, messing with the pieces, taking my time with them. So part of me knows that was real.\n\nBut right now it feels very far away. It doesn’t feel like, “Oh, I’ll get back to that soon.” It feels more like I’m looking at proof that I used to care about things. The kits are still sitting there, and I’ll see them and think, “Those are mine,” but they almost feel like they belong to somebody else. Like someone else bought them, someone else had the patience for them, someone else got something out of them.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d say gone for good… that sounds too final, and I guess I don’t want to believe that. But it does feel gone right now. I don’t feel the pull toward any of it. I just remember that I used to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about five months, I think. Not just a bad week or something that comes and goes for a day or two. This whole spell has kind of been hanging over me for around five months now.\n\nI didn’t mark it on a calendar or anything, but when I think back, that’s about when I started noticing that the things I used to do just weren’t pulling me in anymore. At first I thought maybe I was just tired or in a rut, and I kept thinking it would pass if I waited it out. But it’s stayed. Like month after month, it’s still been there, that feeling of not really caring about the things I used to enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, yes. It’s not always like I’m crying or anything like that, but I wake up and there’s just this heavy, empty feeling sitting in my chest right away. Like before anything has even happened, it already feels like the day is going to be something I have to get through.\n\nIt’s hard to explain, because it’s not one clear thought all the time. It’s more like this weight, like nothing good is really waiting for me. I’ll open my eyes and I just feel… low. Empty. Like I want to keep my head down and not really deal with anything or anyone.\n\nAnd that feeling stays with me for most of the day. Sometimes it might fade into the background a little, but it doesn’t really leave. It’s just there, like I’m carrying it around.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have a lot of energy most days?\n\nPatient: No… not really. Most days it feels like I barely have any energy at all. Even little things feel way bigger than they should.\n\nLike taking out the trash — that sounds like nothing, right? It’s just tying the bag, walking it out, coming back in. But I’ll stand there looking at it like it’s this whole job I don’t know if I can do. Or going to the clinic, even when I know I need to, it feels like too much before I even start getting ready.\n\nIt’s like my body is heavy in a way that’s hard to explain. My arms and legs feel like they’re full of wet sand, like every movement takes more effort than it should. I’m not talking about being a little tired after doing something. It’s more like I wake up already drained, and then everything I’m supposed to do just sits there waiting on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t have a scale at home, so I can’t tell you the exact number, but I can see it in my clothes. My pants are loose on me now, like they kind of hang off my waist more than they used to.\n\nAnd it’s probably because I’m just not eating right. Some days I’ll realize it’s already late and all I’ve had is coffee, maybe a piece of bread or something small like that. It’s not like I’m trying to diet or anything. Food just doesn’t feel… appealing, I guess. I don’t really get myself to make a meal, and even when there’s something there, I’ll take a few bites and stop.\n\nSo yeah, over these past few months, I’ve definitely gotten thinner. Enough that I notice it when I put my pants on.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think I have. It’s like the things I used to do because they made me feel calm or interested, they just don’t do anything for me now.

I used to like walking by the lake. Not even for exercise exactly, just… being there, looking at the water, hearing it move a little, watching people go by. It used to make me feel like I could breathe for a while. Now I’ll think about going, and it feels like there’s no point. Like I already know I’m not going to enjoy it, so I don’t even get myself out there.

And I used to work on these little model kits too. I had a couple I was putting together slowly, and I liked getting the pieces right, painting little parts, taking my time with it. But now they just sit there. I’ll look at the box or the half-finished one on the table, and it almost feels like it belongs to somebody else. Like I remember that I used to care about it, but I can’t feel that anymore.

It’s not that I decided I don’t like those things. It’s more like the part of me that used to enjoy them just isn’t really there right now.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… definitely more than two weeks. It’s been going on for months now.

I’d say this spell, this low feeling, has been hanging over me for about five months. It’s hard because after a while it stops feeling like a “bad week” or a rough patch, and it just becomes… the way the days are. I keep thinking maybe it’ll lift on its own, like maybe next week I’ll feel more normal, but then another week goes by and it’s the same heaviness still sitting there.

So yes, it’s been way longer than two weeks. About five months, I think.

It’s more that since I started barely eating—some days just coffee, maybe a piece of bread later—it feels like the weight’s coming off whether I mean for it to or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no appetite most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the time I really don’t have much of an appetite. It’s like food just doesn’t call to me anymore, if that makes sense. I’ll get through a whole part of the day and then realize all I’ve had is coffee, and maybe later I’ll force down something small, like a piece of bread, just because I know I probably should.\n\nIt’s not like I’m planning it or trying to not eat. I just… hardly eat right anymore. And it’s gotten noticeable because my pants are hanging loose on me now, like I keep having to pull them up. So yeah, I’d say most of the time my appetite is pretty much gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in food in general?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. I wouldn’t say food disgusts me or anything like that, it’s just… I don’t really think about it much, and when I do, it feels like a chore. Like something I’m supposed to do, not something I want.\n\nThat’s kind of what brought me in, honestly. I hardly eat right anymore. Some days it’s just coffee, and then maybe way later I’ll have a piece of bread or something small, just to have something in me. And I keep noticing my pants hanging loose, like they don’t fit the same, so it’s not just in my head. I’m not trying to make that happen, I just don’t seem interested in food like I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in food is related to anything going on in your life or your mind?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know exactly what it’s tied to. It feels like something in me just shut off around food. Like I’ll think, “I should probably eat,” but there’s no pull to actually do it. It’s not like I sat down and decided not to eat, or I’m trying to lose weight. It just happens.\n\nWhat really got my attention is I hardly eat right anymore. Some days I’ll have coffee and that’s basically it for a long while, and then maybe later I’ll have a piece of bread or something small because I know I need something in my stomach. And then I notice my pants hanging loose on me, like I’m constantly pulling them up, and that makes it feel more real.\n\nSo yeah, I guess it must be related to whatever’s going on with me, because this isn’t how I normally am. But I can’t point to one thing and say, “That’s why.” It’s more like my mind just isn’t interested in food right now.\n\nDoctor: Are there other things you used to enjoy that you don't find pleasurable anymore?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, there are. I used to like walking by the lake. Not even anything big, just going there and taking my time, watching the water, hearing the birds, feeling like I could breathe a little. I used to look forward to that, like it was one of the few simple things that made me feel okay.\n\nAnd I had these little model kits too—nothing fancy, just stuff I’d work on with my hands. I’d sit there lining up the pieces, painting small parts, taking my time with it. It used to calm me down. But now they just sit there. I see them on the table or in the corner, and it’s like… I don’t know, like they belong to somebody else. Like I recognize that I used to care about them, but I can’t reach that feeling anymore.\n\nI’ll think, “Maybe I should go walk,” or “Maybe I should pick up one of those kits,” but then I just don’t. There’s no pleasure in the thought of it. It all feels kind of flat.\n\nDoctor: Are there other things besides food and the activities you mentioned, like walking or model kits, that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, the main ones that come to mind are still those same things, honestly. The walking by the lake and the model kits, because those were sort of my things. I didn’t have a whole big list of hobbies, you know? It wasn’t like I was out doing all kinds of stuff before. But those mattered to me.\n\nThe lake especially… I used to like just being there. I’d go and walk slow, sometimes sit for a while, just look at the water. It sounds small, but it used to give me something. And the little model kits, I’d have them set up and I could spend a long time on one tiny part, and it felt satisfying when it came together.\n\nNow they just sit there. I’ll see the kits sitting around and it’s like they belong to somebody else. Like I know I’m the one who bought them, I know I used to enjoy doing them, but I don’t feel pulled toward them anymore. Same with walking—I can think about it, but I don’t really want to go. It’s like the enjoyment part is missing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you feel like doing those things you used to enjoy, like walking by the lake or working on your model kits?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’ll have the thought sometimes, like, “You used to go down to the lake, maybe you should do that,” or I’ll see the model kits sitting there and think maybe I should pick one up. But it’s not the same as wanting to do it.\n\nBefore, I would actually feel something about it, like the idea of walking by the water would feel kind of nice in my head before I even went. Or with the kits, I’d get interested in lining things up or figuring out the little pieces. Now it’s more like I’m just remembering that I used to be a person who liked those things.\n\nThey just sit there now. The model kits especially… I look at them and it’s strange, like they belong to somebody else, even though I know they’re mine. And the lake is the same way in my mind. I know I used to like walking there, but I don’t really get that pull to go. So no, I don’t really have days where I feel like doing them. It’s more just me knowing I should maybe try, but there’s nothing behind it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or empty inside?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, honestly. It’s not always like crying sad, if that makes sense. Sometimes it’s more just this heavy, empty feeling, like I wake up and before anything even happens, it’s already sitting there in my chest.\n\nIt feels like there’s nothing good waiting for me. Like the day is already kind of… blank, or weighed down, before I even get out of bed. I just want to keep my head down and not deal with much. It’s hard to explain, because “sad” sounds like one thing, but for me it’s more like everything inside feels hollow and heavy at the same time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep has been pretty bad lately. It’s like I’m tired, but then when I actually get in bed, my body won’t just shut off. I’ll lie there for hours sometimes, just staring at the wall or the ceiling, turning over, trying to get comfortable, and nothing really happens. It gets quiet and dark, and I’m just… awake.\n\nAnd then other times, even if I do fall asleep, I wake up way too early, like before the sun is even up, and I just can’t get back to sleep. I’ll keep thinking maybe if I stay still long enough I’ll drift off again, but I don’t. I just lay there knowing the whole day is going to start and I already feel worn out before it even begins.\n\nEven on nights where I technically slept some, it doesn’t feel like it helped much. I get up feeling like I barely rested at all. It’s frustrating, because I want to sleep, I really do, but it’s either taking forever to come or it doesn’t last.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you might enjoy those things again, like walking by the lake or working on your model kits, or does it feel like that part of you is gone for good?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I want to say maybe, because I remember that I used to be that way. Like, I remember walking by the lake and it actually meaning something to me, just being there by the water and having that quiet feeling. And I remember sitting with those little model kits, messing with the pieces, taking my time with them. So part of me knows that was real.\n\nBut right now it feels very far away. It doesn’t feel like, “Oh, I’ll get back to that soon.” It feels more like I’m looking at proof that I used to care about things. The kits are still sitting there, and I’ll see them and think, “Those are mine,” but they almost feel like they belong to somebody else. Like someone else bought them, someone else had the patience for them, someone else got something out of them.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d say gone for good… that sounds too final, and I guess I don’t want to believe that. But it does feel gone right now. I don’t feel the pull toward any of it. I just remember that I used to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about five months, I think. Not just a bad week or something that comes and goes for a day or two. This whole spell has kind of been hanging over me for around five months now.\n\nI didn’t mark it on a calendar or anything, but when I think back, that’s about when I started noticing that the things I used to do just weren’t pulling me in anymore. At first I thought maybe I was just tired or in a rut, and I kept thinking it would pass if I waited it out. But it’s stayed. Like month after month, it’s still been there, that feeling of not really caring about the things I used to enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, yes. It’s not always like I’m crying or anything like that, but I wake up and there’s just this heavy, empty feeling sitting in my chest right away. Like before anything has even happened, it already feels like the day is going to be something I have to get through.\n\nIt’s hard to explain, because it’s not one clear thought all the time. It’s more like this weight, like nothing good is really waiting for me. I’ll open my eyes and I just feel… low. Empty. Like I want to keep my head down and not really deal with anything or anyone.\n\nAnd that feeling stays with me for most of the day. Sometimes it might fade into the background a little, but it doesn’t really leave. It’s just there, like I’m carrying it around.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have a lot of energy most days?\n\nPatient: No… not really. Most days it feels like I barely have any energy at all. Even little things feel way bigger than they should.\n\nLike taking out the trash — that sounds like nothing, right? It’s just tying the bag, walking it out, coming back in. But I’ll stand there looking at it like it’s this whole job I don’t know if I can do. Or going to the clinic, even when I know I need to, it feels like too much before I even start getting ready.\n\nIt’s like my body is heavy in a way that’s hard to explain. My arms and legs feel like they’re full of wet sand, like every movement takes more effort than it should. I’m not talking about being a little tired after doing something. It’s more like I wake up already drained, and then everything I’m supposed to do just sits there waiting on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t have a scale at home, so I can’t tell you the exact number, but I can see it in my clothes. My pants are loose on me now, like they kind of hang off my waist more than they used to.\n\nAnd it’s probably because I’m just not eating right. Some days I’ll realize it’s already late and all I’ve had is coffee, maybe a piece of bread or something small like that. It’s not like I’m trying to diet or anything. Food just doesn’t feel… appealing, I guess. I don’t really get myself to make a meal, and even when there’s something there, I’ll take a few bites and stop.\n\nSo yeah, over these past few months, I’ve definitely gotten thinner. Enough that I notice it when I put my pants on.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost pleasure in almost all activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have. It’s like the things I used to do because they made me feel calm or interested, they just don’t do anything for me now.\n\nI used to like walking by the lake. Not even for exercise exactly, just… being there, looking at the water, hearing it move a little, watching people go by. It used to make me feel like I could breathe for a while. Now I’ll think about going, and it feels like there’s no point. Like I already know I’m not going to enjoy it, so I don’t even get myself out there.\n\nAnd I used to work on these little model kits too. I had a couple I was putting together slowly, and I liked getting the pieces right, painting little parts, taking my time with it. But now they just sit there. I’ll look at the box or the half-finished one on the table, and it almost feels like it belongs to somebody else. Like I remember that I used to care about it, but I can’t feel that anymore.\n\nIt’s not that I decided I don’t like those things. It’s more like the part of me that used to enjoy them just isn’t really there right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely more than two weeks. It’s been going on for months now.\n\nI’d say this spell, this low feeling, has been hanging over me for about five months. It’s hard because after a while it stops feeling like a “bad week” or a rough patch, and it just becomes… the way the days are. I keep thinking maybe it’ll lift on its own, like maybe next week I’ll feel more normal, but then another week goes by and it’s the same heaviness still sitting there.\n\nSo yes, it’s been way longer than two weeks. About five months, I think."
